The \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {bms}$$\end{document}bms-modules \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}, c_L, c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) induced from \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{\Bbbk }_{\textbf{M}, \textbf{s},c_L,c_M}$$\end{document}k~M,s,cL,cM are called massive modules [8, 22, 23, 34]. That is,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}, c_L, c_M) :={{\,\textrm{Ind}\,}}_{\widehat{\mathfrak {h}}}^{\mathfrak {bms}} \widetilde{\Bbbk }_{\textbf{M},\textbf{s},c_L,c_M}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM):=Indh^bmsk~M,s,cL,cM.

The massive module \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}, c_L, c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) admits a PBW basis of monomials\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} L_{n_k} \dots L_{n_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Lnk⋯Ln1|M,s⟩,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n_1,\dots , n_k \in \mathbb {Z}\setminus \{0\}$$\end{document}n1,⋯,nk∈Z\{0} with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n_k \ge \dots \ge n_1$$\end{document}nk≥⋯≥n1.

The following result characterises the reducibility of the massive modules in terms of the parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M},\textbf{s},c_L,c_M$$\end{document}M,s,cL,cM. To the best of our knowledge, this result has not been explicitly stated and proven in the literature before.

Theorem 3.7

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M},\textbf{s},c_L,c_M \in \Bbbk $$\end{document}M,s,cL,cM∈k. The massive \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {bms}$$\end{document}bms-module \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M},\textbf{s},c_L,c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) is irreducible if and only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M}+\frac{n^2-1}{24}c_M\ne 0$$\end{document}M+n2-124cM≠0 for any positive integer n.

Proof

We would like to apply Theorem 2.15. Hence, we first aim to relate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M},\textbf{s},c_L,c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) to a universal quasi-Whittaker module of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {bms}$$\end{document}bms as per Definition 2.12. Consider the ideal \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {a}:={{\,\textrm{span}\,}}\{C_M, C_L, M_k \mid k \in \mathbb {Z}\}$$\end{document}a:=span{CM,CL,Mk∣k∈Z} of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {bms}$$\end{document}bms and define the Whittaker function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi :\mathfrak {a}\rightarrow \Bbbk $$\end{document}ϕ:a→k given by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \phi (M_i) = \delta _{i,0} \textbf{M}, \quad \phi (C_L) = c_L, \quad \phi (C_M) = c_M. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ϕ(Mi)=δi,0M,ϕ(CL)=cL,ϕ(CM)=cM.Letting \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W_\phi :={{\,\textrm{Ind}\,}}_\mathfrak {a}^\mathfrak {bms}\Bbbk _\phi $$\end{document}Wϕ:=Indabmskϕ be the associated universal quasi-Whittaker module, it is easy to see that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s},c_L,c_M) \cong W_\phi /J_{\textbf{s}}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM)≅Wϕ/Js,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J_{\textbf{s}} :=U(\mathfrak {bms})(L_0 - \textbf{s}) \cdot 1_\phi $$\end{document}Js:=U(bms)(L0-s)·1ϕ.

Suppose first that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M} + \frac{n^2-1}{24}c_M \ne 0$$\end{document}M+n2-124cM≠0 for any positive integer n. By setting n = 1n=1, we see that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M} \ne 0$$\end{document}M≠0. From Definition 2.14, we may quickly deduce that the Whittaker annihilator of ϕ ϕ is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {a}\oplus \Bbbk L_0$$\end{document}a⊕kL0. Now, Theorem 2.15 implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J_{\textbf{s}}$$\end{document}Js is a maximal \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {bms}$$\end{document}bms-submodule of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W_\phi $$\end{document}Wϕ. Therefore, the quotient \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s},c_L,c_M) \cong W_\phi /J_{\textbf{s}}$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM)≅Wϕ/Js is irreducible.

Conversely, assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M} + \frac{n^2 - 1}{24} c_M = 0$$\end{document}M+n2-124cM=0 for a positive integer n. It is easy to compute that the Whittaker annihilator of ϕ ϕ contains \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {a}\oplus \Bbbk L_0 \oplus \Bbbk L_n \oplus \Bbbk L_{-n}$$\end{document}a⊕kL0⊕kLn⊕kL-n. Therefore, we see that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_{n}|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}Ln|M,s⟩ generates a nonzero proper submodule of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s},c_L,c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM). Thus, in this case, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s},c_L,c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) is reducible. □ □

Remark 3.8

By comparing [57, Theorem 2.3] with Theorem 3.7, we see that the massive modules \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}, c_L, c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) are irreducible under the same conditions as the Verma modules of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {bms}$$\end{document}bms from Definition 3.4. This is an unexpected, intriguing similarity between two very different classes of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {bms}$$\end{document}bms-modules that could potentially be explained by structural features of these modules that are yet to be discovered.

Using the findings from the proof of Theorem 3.7 and another result from [32], it is not difficult to show that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_n |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}Ln|M,s⟩ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_{-n} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}L-n|M,s⟩ together generate a maximal submodule of the massive module \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}, c_L, c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M} + \frac{n^2 - 1}{24}c_M = \textbf{s} + \frac{n^2 - 1}{24}c_L = 0$$\end{document}M+n2-124cM=s+n2-124cL=0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_M \ne 0$$\end{document}cM≠0.

Corollary 3.9

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}, c_L, c_M \in \Bbbk $$\end{document}M,s,cL,cM∈k with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_M \ne 0$$\end{document}cM≠0, and suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M} + \frac{n^2 - 1}{24}c_M = \textbf{s} + \frac{n^2 - 1}{24} c_L = 0$$\end{document}M+n2-124cM=s+n2-124cL=0 for some positive integer n. Then, the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {bms}$$\end{document}bms-submodule of the massive module \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}, c_L, c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) generated by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_n|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}Ln|M,s⟩ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_{-n}|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}L-n|M,s⟩ is a maximal submodule of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}, c_L, c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM).

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi :\mathfrak {a}\rightarrow \Bbbk $$\end{document}ϕ:a→k be as in the proof of Theorem 3.7. Let\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \widetilde{\mathfrak {a}} :={{\,\textrm{span}\,}}\{L_0, L_n, L_{-n}, M_k, C_M, C_L \mid k \in \mathbb {Z}\} = \mathfrak {a}\oplus \Bbbk L_0 \oplus \Bbbk L_n \oplus \Bbbk L_{-n}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}a~:=span{L0,Ln,L-n,Mk,CM,CL∣k∈Z}=a⊕kL0⊕kLn⊕kL-n,and define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi :\widetilde{\mathfrak {a}} \rightarrow \Bbbk $$\end{document}Φ:a~→k by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${ \left. \hspace{0.0pt}\Phi \phantom {\big |} \right| _{\mathfrak {a}} } = \phi $$\end{document}Φa=ϕ, and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Phi (L_0) = \textbf{s}, \quad \Phi (L_n) = \Phi (L_{-n}) = 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Φ(L0)=s,Φ(Ln)=Φ(L-n)=0.One can compute that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {bms}^\Phi = \widetilde{\mathfrak {a}}$$\end{document}bmsΦ=a~. The condition that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{s} + \frac{n^2 - 1}{24}c_L = 0$$\end{document}s+n2-124cL=0 guarantees that Φ Φ is a well-defined Lie algebra homomorphism, since\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Phi ([L_n, L_{-n}]) = \Phi \left( 2n L_0 + \frac{n^3 - n}{12} C_L\right) = 2n \textbf{s} + \frac{n^3 - n}{12} c_L = 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Φ([Ln,L-n])=Φ2nL0+n3-n12CL=2ns+n3-n12cL=0.Therefore, Φ Φ extends the quasi-Whittaker function ϕ ϕ to the entirety of its Whittaker annihilator. By [32, Lemma 3.7], it follows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{Ind}\,}}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak {a}}}^\mathfrak {bms}\Bbbk _{\Phi }$$\end{document}Inda~bmskΦ is an irreducible \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {bms}$$\end{document}bms-module. It is easy to see that this module is isomorphic to the quotient of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}, c_L, c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) by its submodule generated by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_n|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}Ln|M,s⟩ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_{-n}|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}L-n|M,s⟩, yielding the result. □ □

Remark 3.10

We were informed by Blagoje Oblak that Theorem 3.7 and Corollary 3.9 match the intuitive expectation stemming from his work [23] with Glenn Barnich, in which the authors study coadjoint orbits of the BMS3 group and provide classical and quantum mechanical interpretations of these findings in the context of three-dimensional asymptotically flat gravity. It is reassuring that we are able to make this physical intuition mathematically precise.

We now consider the reducibility of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^-submodule of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}, c_L, c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) generated by the element \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}|M,s⟩. The condition governing its reducibility turns out to be exactly the same as the one in Theorem 3.7, and the proof is easier, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U(\widehat{\mathfrak {b}})|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}U(b^)|M,s⟩ is a quasi-Whittaker module over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^ on the nose.

Proposition 3.11

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M}, \textbf{s},c_L,c_M \in \Bbbk $$\end{document}M,s,cL,cM∈k. The \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^-module \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U(\widehat{\mathfrak {b}})|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}U(b^)|M,s⟩ is irreducible if and only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M} + \frac{n^2 - 1}{24}c_M \ne 0$$\end{document}M+n2-124cM≠0 for any positive integer n.

Proof

Suppose first that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M} +\frac{n^2-1}{24}c_M\ne 0$$\end{document}M+n2-124cM≠0 for any positive integer n. Again, setting n=1n=1 lets us infer that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M} \ne 0$$\end{document}M≠0. Take \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {a}:={{\,\textrm{span}\,}}\{C_M, P_k:k\ge 0\}$$\end{document}a:=span{CM,Pk:k≥0}, which is an ideal of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^, and define a linear map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi :\mathfrak {a}\rightarrow \Bbbk $$\end{document}ϕ:a→k by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \phi (P_i) = 2\delta _{i,0}\textbf{M}, \quad \phi (C_M) = c_M. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ϕ(Pi)=2δi,0M,ϕ(CM)=cM.The Whittaker annihilator of ϕ ϕ, denoted \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}^\phi $$\end{document}b^ϕ, is easily computed to be \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}^\phi = \mathfrak {a}$$\end{document}b^ϕ=a. It now follows immediately from Theorem 2.15 that the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^-module \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U(\widehat{\mathfrak {b}})|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle \cong W_\phi = {{\,\textrm{Ind}\,}}_\mathfrak {a}^{\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}} \Bbbk _\phi $$\end{document}U(b^)|M,s⟩≅Wϕ=Indab^kϕ is irreducible.

Conversely, assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M} +\frac{n^2 - 1}{24}c_M = 0$$\end{document}M+n2-124cM=0 for a positive integer n. In this case, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}^\phi \supseteq \mathfrak {a}\oplus \Bbbk \mathcal {O}_n$$\end{document}b^ϕ⊇a⊕kOn. Then, from Theorem 2.15, we see that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {O}_{n}|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}On|M,s⟩ generates a nonzero proper submodule of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U(\widehat{\mathfrak {b}})|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}U(b^)|M,s⟩. Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U(\widehat{\mathfrak {b}})|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}U(b^)|M,s⟩ is reducible in this case. □ □

Thus, the massive modules of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {bms}$$\end{document}bms seem to give rise to irreducible \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^-modules more readily than the Verma modules.

We finish our analysis of massive modules by studying the full space \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M},\textbf{s},c_L,c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) as a module over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^. In determining the structure \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M},\textbf{s},c_L,c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) as a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^-module, we assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M} + \frac{n^2 - 1}{24}c_M \ne 0$$\end{document}M+n2-124cM≠0 for any positive integer n for the rest of this section, meaning that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U(\widehat{\mathfrak {b}})|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}U(b^)|M,s⟩ is irreducible. We first introduce two new symbols.

Notation 3.12

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}n∈Z, define\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \widehat{P}_n :=M_n - M_{-n}, \quad \widehat{\mathcal {O}}_n :=L_n + L_{-n}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}P^n:=Mn-M-n,O^n:=Ln+L-n,as elements of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {bms}$$\end{document}bms. Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{P}_n$$\end{document}P^n and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathcal {O}}_n$$\end{document}O^n are not elements of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^.

By (2.1), a simple computation shows that for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n,m \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}n,m∈N we have3.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&{[}P_n, \widehat{\mathcal {O}}_m] = (n - m)\widehat{P}_{n + m} + (n + m)\widehat{P}_{n - m}, \\&{[}\widehat{P}_n, \widehat{\mathcal {O}}_m] = (n - m){P}_{n + m} + (n + m){P}_{n - m} + \frac{1}{6} n (n^2 - 1)\delta _{n,m} C_M. \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}[Pn,O^m]=(n-m)P^n+m+(n+m)P^n-m,[P^n,O^m]=(n-m)Pn+m+(n+m)Pn-m+16n(n2-1)δn,mCM.Notice that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{\mathcal {O}_n, \widehat{\mathcal {O}}_m, P_m, \widehat{P}_n \mid n \in \mathbb {Z}_+, m \in \mathbb {N}\}$$\end{document}{On,O^m,Pm,P^n∣n∈Z+,m∈N} is another basis of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {bms}$$\end{document}bms. Hence, by the PBW theorem, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}, c_L, c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) has a basis given by monomials of the form3.3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {O}_{p_k}^{q_k} \dots \mathcal {O}_{p_1}^{q_1} \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_\ell }_{r_\ell } \dots {\widehat{\mathcal {O}}}^{s_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Opkk⋯Op11O^ℓℓ⋯O^11|M,s⟩,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p_k> \dots > p_1 \ge 1$$\end{document}pk>⋯>p1≥1, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_\ell> \dots > r_1 \ge 1$$\end{document}rℓ>⋯>r1≥1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q_1, \dots , q_k, s_1, \dots , s_\ell \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}q1,⋯,qk,s1,⋯,sℓ∈N.

We now prove that the element \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathcal {O}}_n |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}O^n|M,s⟩ of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}, c_L, c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) generates an irreducible \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^-submodule, for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \mathbb {Z}_+$$\end{document}n∈Z+.

Proposition 3.13

For any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \mathbb {Z}_+$$\end{document}n∈Z+, the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^-module \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U(\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}) \widehat{\mathcal {O}}_n |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}U(b^)O^n|M,s⟩ is isomorphic to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U(\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}) |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}U(b^)|M,s⟩. Consequently, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U(\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}) \widehat{\mathcal {O}}_n |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}U(b^)O^n|M,s⟩ is an irreducible \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^-module.

Proof

It follows from (3.2) that for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}r∈N,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} P_r \widehat{\mathcal {O}}_n |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle = \delta _{0,r} 2M |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}PrO^n|M,s⟩=δ0,r2M|M,s⟩.As above, the PBW theorem implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U(\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}) \widehat{\mathcal {O}}_n |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}U(b^)O^n|M,s⟩ has a basis given by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {O}_{p_k}^{q_k} \dots \mathcal {O}_{p_1}^{q_1} \widehat{\mathcal {O}}_n|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Opkk⋯Op11O^n|M,s⟩,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p_k> \dots > p_1 \ge 1$$\end{document}pk>⋯>p1≥1, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q_1, \dots , q_k\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}q1,⋯,qk∈N. Therefore, we see that the map\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} U(\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}) \widehat{\mathcal {O}}_n |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle&\rightarrow U(\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}) |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle \\ \mathcal {O}_{p_k}^{q_k} \dots \mathcal {O}_{p_1}^{q_1} \widehat{\mathcal {O}}_n |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle&\mapsto \mathcal {O}_{p_k}^{q_k} \dots \mathcal {O}_{p_1}^{q_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}U(b^)O^n|M,s⟩→U(b^)|M,s⟩Opkk⋯Op11O^n|M,s⟩↦Opkk⋯Op11|M,s⟩,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p_k> \dots > p_1 \ge 1$$\end{document}pk>⋯>p1≥1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q_1, \dots , q_k \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}q1,⋯,qk∈N, is an isomorphism of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^-modules.

The final sentence follows from Proposition 3.11 upon recalling that we are assuming that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{M} + \frac{n^2 - 1}{24}c_M \ne 0$$\end{document}M+n2-124cM≠0. □ □

It is not surprising that the massive \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {bms}$$\end{document}bms-module \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}, c_L, c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) does not remain irreducible when viewed as a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^-module. What is perhaps more surprising is that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{V}(\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}, c_L, c_M)$$\end{document}V~(M,s,cL,cM) decomposes into a direct sum of two \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathfrak {b}}$$\end{document}b^-submodules, as we will soon see. We define these submodules below.

Definition 3.14

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{V}$$\end{document}V^ be the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}O^ℓℓ⋯O^11|M,s⟩∣ℓ∈N,rℓ>⋯>r1≥1,s1+⋯+sℓ=1(mod2).

The following result shows that the action of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_r$$\end{document}Pr on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_\ell }_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}O^ℓℓ⋯O^11|M,s⟩ preserves the parity of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum s_i$$\end{document}∑si.

Proposition 3.15

For any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r, s_1,\ldots , s_{\ell }\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}r,s1,…,sℓ∈N and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_\ell> \dots > r_1 \ge 1$$\end{document}rℓ>⋯>r1≥1, the abelian part of the BMS3 algebra has the following action on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_\ell }_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}O^ℓℓ⋯O^11|M,s⟩:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} P_r \cdot \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_\ell }_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle&= \delta _{0,r} 2\textbf{M} \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_\ell }_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle&\pmod {\bigoplus _{\begin{array}{c} 0 \le u_i \le s_i, \sum u_i < \sum s_i, \\ \sum (s_i - u_i) = 0 \pmod {2} \end{array}} \Bbbk \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{u_\ell }_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{u_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle }, \\ \widehat{P}_r \cdot \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_\ell }_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle&= 0&\pmod {\bigoplus _{\begin{array}{c} 0 \le u_i \le s_i, \\ \sum (s_i - u_i) = 1 \pmod {2} \end{array}} \Bbbk \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{u_\ell }_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{u_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle }. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pr·O^ℓℓ⋯O^11|M,s⟩=δ0,r2MO^ℓℓ⋯O^11|M,s⟩(mod⨁0≤ui≤si,∑ui<∑si,∑(si-ui)=0(mod2)kO^ℓℓ⋯O^11|M,s⟩),P^r·O^ℓℓ⋯O^11|M,s⟩=0(mod⨁0≤ui≤si,∑(si-ui)=1(mod2)kO^ℓℓ⋯O^11|M,s⟩).

Proof

We prove the result by induction on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d :=\sum _{i = 1}^{\ell } s_i$$\end{document}d:=∑i=1ℓsi. It is not difficult to check that the two equalities hold for d = 0, 1d=0,1. Now assume that d>1d>1 and the two equalities hold for all 0 ≤ d'< d0≤d′<d. Consider the first equality for the case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d = \sum _{i = 1}^{\ell } s_i$$\end{document}d=∑i=1ℓsi. We have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&P_r \cdot \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_\ell }_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle = [P_r, \widehat{\mathcal {O}}_{r_\ell }] \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_\ell -1}_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle + \widehat{\mathcal {O}}_{r_\ell } P_r\widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_\ell - 1}_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle \\&\quad = \left( (r - r_{\ell }) \widehat{P}_{r + r_{\ell }} + (r + r_{\ell }) \widehat{P}_{r - r_{\ell }} + \widehat{\mathcal {O}}_{r_\ell } P_r\right) \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_\ell - 1}_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pr·O^ℓℓ⋯O^11|M,s⟩=[Pr,O^rℓ]O^ℓsℓ-1⋯O^11|M,s⟩+O^rℓPrO^ℓsℓ-1⋯O^11|M,s⟩=(r-rℓ)P^r+rℓ+(r+rℓ)P^r-rℓ+O^rℓPrO^ℓsℓ-1⋯O^11|M,s⟩.The induction hypothesis implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left( (r - r_{\ell }) \widehat{P}_{r + r_{\ell }} + (r + r_{\ell }) \widehat{P}_{r - r_{\ell }}\right) \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_\ell - 1}_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}(r-rℓ)P^r+rℓ+(r+rℓ)P^r-rℓO^ℓsℓ-1⋯O^11|M,s⟩ belongs to\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bigoplus _{\begin{array}{c} 0 \le u_i \le s_i - \delta _{i,\ell }, \\ \sum (s_i - u_i) - 1 = 1 \pmod {2} \end{array}} \Bbbk \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{u_\ell }_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{u_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle \subseteq \bigoplus _{\begin{array}{c} 0 \le u_i \le s_i, \sum u_i < \sum s_i, \\ \sum (s_i - u_i) = 0 \pmod {2} \end{array}} \Bbbk \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{u_\ell }_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{u_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle ,$$\end{document}⨁0≤ui≤si-δi,ℓ,∑(si-ui)-1=1(mod2)kO^ℓℓ⋯O^11|M,s⟩⊆⨁0≤ui≤si,∑ui<∑si,∑(si-ui)=0(mod2)kO^ℓℓ⋯O^11|M,s⟩,and that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_r\widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_\ell -1}_{r_\ell }\dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_1}_{r_1}|\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle - \delta _{0,r} 2\textbf{M} \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_\ell -1}_{r_\ell } \dots \widehat{\mathcal {O}}^{s_1}_{r_1} |\textbf{M}, \textbf{s}\rangle $$\end{document}PrO^ℓsℓ-1⋯O^11|M,s⟩-δ0,r2MO^ℓsℓ-1⋯O^11|M,s⟩ belongs to\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bigoplus _ {\begin{array}{c} 0 \le u_i \le s_i-\delta _{i,\ell }, \sum u_i < \sum s_i -
